- **biomedical-nlp-eval**: This benchmark evaluates large language models on four core biomedical natural language processing tasks: event extraction, relation extraction, named entity recognition, and text classification. - **biomedical-paper**: AI-powered biomedical manuscript generation with docx output. Activates when user provides Chinese draft/outline and requests full English research paper. Includes: Abstract, Introduction, Methods, Results, Discussion, References. - **bioproject-search**: Retrieves NCBI BioProject metadata — title, description, organism, submitter and release dates — for a batch of BioProject accessions, using the official NCBI E-utilities API rather than scraping the BioProject web pages. Supports keyword filtering over titles and descriptions, CSV/JSON export, and requires a contact email as the NCBI usage policy mandates. This skill should be used when looking up what a BioProject or PRJNA/PRD accession contains, retrieving sequencing project descriptions, or collecting BioProject metadata in bulk is requested. - **biopulse-qa-eval**: This benchmark evaluates large language models on biomedical question-answering, specifically probing their factuality, robustness to linguistic variations (paraphrasing and typos), and susceptibility to demographic bias (age and gender). It distinguishes between extractive and abstractive reasoning capabilities using expert-verified QA pairs from clinical documents. Use when the user wants to benchmark on BioPulse-QA, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports F1. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/biopulse-qa-eval) · ★3)
- **biopython**: Biopython is a comprehensive set of freely available Python tools for biological computation. It provides functionality for sequence manipulation, file I/O, database access, structural bioinformatics, phylogenetics, and many other bioinformatics tasks. ([sickn33/agentic-awesome-skills](https://github.com/sickn33/agentic-awesome-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/agentic-awesome-skills-claude/skills/biopython) · ★50k · 330 copies)
- **biopython**: Primary Python toolkit for molecular biology. Preferred for Python-based PubMed/NCBI queries (Bio.Entrez), sequence manipulation, file parsing (FASTA, GenBank, FASTQ, PDB), advanced BLAST workflows, structures, phylogenetics. For quick BLAST, use gget. For direct REST API, use pubmed-database. ([davila7/claude-code-templates](https://github.com/davila7/claude-code-templates/tree/HEAD/cli-tool/components/skills/scientific/biopython) · ★34k · 93 copies)
- **biopython**: Comprehensive molecular biology toolkit. Use for sequence manipulation, file parsing (FASTA/GenBank/PDB), phylogenetics, and programmatic NCBI/PubMed access (Bio.Entrez). Best for batch processing, custom bioinformatics pipelines, BLAST automation. For quick lookups use gget; for multi-service integration use bioservices. ([synthetic-sciences/openscience](https://github.com/synthetic-sciences/openscience/tree/HEAD/backend/cli/skills/biology/biopython) · ★4.4k · 199 copies)
- **biopython**: Primary retained Python toolkit for molecular biology sequence work. Preferred for sequence manipulation, FASTA/FASTQ/GenBank parsing, Bio.Entrez, BLAST workflows, alignments, structures, and phylogenetics. For biological database evidence lookup, use bio-database-evidence. For single-cell workflows use scanpy. For direct literature REST API, use pubmed-database. ([foryourhealth111-pixel/Vibe-Skills](https://github.com/foryourhealth111-pixel/Vibe-Skills/tree/HEAD/bundled/skills/biopython) · ★3.8k · 2 copies)
- **biopython**: A comprehensive toolbox for computational molecular biology; use it when you need programmatic sequence/structure parsing, batch bioinformatics pipelines, or automated NCBI/BLAST workflows. ([aipoch/medical-research-skills](https://github.com/aipoch/medical-research-skills/tree/HEAD/scientific-skills/Data%20Analysis/biopython) · ★2.1k)

- **biorecovery-audit**: Audit sleep, hydration, and nutrition metrics to generate a recovery score that gates training intensity and prevents overtraining. - **bioresearch-agent-router**: Classify a free-text research intent into the right BioResearch Agent workflow (literature / biomarker / causal / case-study / doctor) using a deterministic, rule-based router. - **bioresearch-ancestry-aware-mr**: Run cross-ancestry Mendelian randomization with correlated-pleiotropy modeling (CAUSE-like) and mixture-model pleiotropy detection (MRMix-like), plus per-ancestry IVW, cross-ancestry meta-analysis (FE/RE), and portability assessment. Use when the user needs to test whether a causal effect estimated in one ancestry transfers to others, or to distinguish true causation from correlated horizontal pleiotropy. Mock mode validates the MR + pleiotropy pipeline; live mode would use BBJ/FinnGen/TPMI/UKB summary statistics. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/biomedical/ancestry-aware-mr) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-biomarker-discovery**: Run reproducible biomarker discovery via the BioResearch Agent biomarker workflow (differential expression, pathway enrichment, candidate ranking, volcano plot) over a GEO dataset or synthetic data. Use when the user asks to find gene biomarkers or candidate genes for a disease. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/biomedical/biomarker-discovery) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-causal-evidence**: Run the causal-evidence chain (GWAS → eQTL → colocalization → TWAS → fine-mapping → MR) via the BioResearch Agent causal-evidence workflow. Use when the user wants to test whether a GWAS risk locus acts through a candidate gene's expression, identify the credible set, and check MR consistency. Synthetic loci by default; methodology validation, not a real etiological claim. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/biomedical/causal-evidence) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-causal-inference**: Run two-sample Mendelian randomization via the BioResearch Agent causal workflow (IVW estimation, leave-one-out sensitivity, scatter / funnel plots). Use when the user asks to test a causal effect between an exposure and an outcome (e.g., BMI → Type 2 Diabetes) using genetic instruments. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/biomedical/causal-inference) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-differential-expression**: Perform differential expression (DEG) analysis via the BioResearch Agent biomarker workflow, which computes DEGs (t-test + Bonferroni correction) as its first stage. Use when the user specifically asks for differential expression, DEGs, fold-change, or a volcano plot for a disease or gene-expression dataset. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/biomedical/differential-expression) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-disease-case-study**: Run a reproducible, real-public-data disease case study that composes the literature → biomarker → causal workflows end-to-end and emits a blind benchmark evaluation (known-gene recovery, pathway sanity, reproducibility hash). Use when the user wants to prove the framework on a real disease (e.g. Parkinson's GSE7621) or asks for a "case study", "validation run", or "benchmark" of the biomedical workflows. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/biomedical/disease-case-study) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-environment-check**: Verify the BioResearch Agent framework installation, Python/dependency versions, network access to biomedical databases, and output-directory permissions. Use when the user asks to validate the environment, troubleshoot a failed workflow run, or before running any analysis. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/core/environment-check) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-foundation-embeddings**: Generate and evaluate single-cell foundation model embeddings (scGPT / UCE / scFoundation). Runs mock-mode pipeline validation by default — simulated embeddings that recover planted cell-type structure. Live mode stubs (pip install scgpt, UCE eval_single_anndata.py, scFoundation get_embedding.py) are documented for real deployment. Use when the user wants to embed scRNA-seq data with foundation models, compare embedding quality across models, or benchmark cell-type recovery. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/biomedical/foundation-embeddings) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-gwas-harmonization**: Harmonize GWAS summary statistics across ancestries for cross-ancestry MR. Use when the user needs to align alleles, flip strands, resolve palindromic SNPs, compute allele-frequency divergence (Fst-like), or identify cross-ancestry genome-wide signal overlap. Supports GWAS-SSF schema; mock mode validates the harmonization pipeline with simulated cross-ancestry GWAS including strand confusion and allele flips. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/biomedical/gwas-harmonization) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-introduction**: Describe the BioResearch Agent Framework and its capabilities to a user or agent. Use when the user asks "what can BioResearch Agent do", wants an overview of available workflows, or is deciding which skill to invoke for a biomedical research task. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/core/project-introduction) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-ld-reference-management**: Manage ancestry-specific LD reference panels and perform LD clumping for cross-ancestry MR. Use when the user needs to simulate ancestry-aware LD block structures (AFR/EUR/EAS/SAS/AMR), run greedy distance-based clumping (PLINK --clump style), or compute LD scores for stratified LDSC. Mock mode validates the clumping pipeline; live mode wraps PLINK 2.0 + 1000 Genomes reference panels. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/biomedical/ld-reference-management) · ★3)
- **bioresearch-literature-analysis**: Run a reproducible biomedical literature review via the BioResearch Agent literature workflow (PubMed retrieval, entity co-occurrence knowledge graph, structured review outline). Use when the user asks for a literature review, research-gap analysis, or a synthesized reading of PubMed papers on a biomedical topic. ([Alim430/bioresearch-agent](https://github.com/Alim430/bioresearch-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/biomedical/literature-analysis) · ★3)

Use when a user wants concise BioStudies summaries ([openai/plugins](https://github.com/openai/plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/life-science-research/skills/biostudies-arrayexpress-skill) · ★8k · 25 copies)
- **biostudies-fetch**: Query metadata and download data from EMBL-EBI BioStudies, the database that describes biological studies and links their data across collections (ArrayExpress, BioImages, BioModels, EGA-linked studies and standalone submissions). Fetch study metadata by accession, list and download attached files, search across collections, and write a harmonised metadata.tsv. ([ClawBio/ClawBio](https://github.com/ClawBio/ClawBio/tree/HEAD/skills/biostudies-fetch) · ★1.2k · 2 copies)
- **biostudies-search**: Discover public life-science studies and associated archive records through EMBL-EBI BioStudies. Use for ArrayExpress, supplementary-data, accession, and public dataset discovery; not as evidence of study quality or reuse permission. ([eightmm/codex-science](https://github.com/eightmm/codex-science/tree/HEAD/authored-skills/biostudies-search) · ★4)
- **BioStudio_Alpha_SC**: Alpha SC combines BioStudio's GPU-native runtime with curated notebooks so you can ingest scRNA-seq, ATAC, and spatial assays without writing boilerplate pipelines. ([mdbabumiamssm/LLMs-Universal-Life-Science-and-Clinical-Skills-](https://github.com/mdbabumiamssm/LLMs-Universal-Life-Science-and-Clinical-Skills-/tree/HEAD/Skills/Genomics/Single_Cell/BioStudio_Alpha_SC) · ★36 · 2 copies)
- **biosymphony**: Use when planning or executing BioSymphony GeneCluster campaigns to find biosynthetic gene clusters and assemble pathway support across genomes and transcriptomes. Applications include bioprospecting toward target molecules, pathway gap-filling, comparative atlas building, and novel-cluster discovery. The skill provides source/query ledgers, route scouting, candidate search, function scoring, claim checks, task contracts, check commands, and provenance. ([BioSymphony/genecluster](https://github.com/BioSymphony/genecluster/tree/HEAD/skills/biosymphony) · ★4)
- **biosymphony-ferm-doe**: Use only for an active BioSymphony Ferm DoE campaign involving fermentation, cell-culture, upstream bioprocess, adaptive DoE, assay-readiness, or pre-experiment planning. ([BioSymphony/ferm-doe](https://github.com/BioSymphony/ferm-doe/tree/HEAD/skills/biosymphony-ferm-doe) · ★1)
- **biosymphony-structure-factory**: Select structural biology tools, call configured adapters, chain stage outputs, and compare computational results. Use for binder-design comparisons, structure prediction, model review, and agent-driven execution. ([BioSymphony/structure-factory](https://github.com/BioSymphony/structure-factory/tree/HEAD/skills/biosymphony-structure-factory) · ★12)
- **Biosync** ([matt-fell-ai/PAI](https://github.com/matt-fell-ai/PAI/tree/HEAD/skills/Biosync) · ★1)
- **biosynformatic-descriptor-computation**: Use when when you have a natural product structure (as a molecule object, SMILES string, or InChI string) and need to generate a biosynformatic descriptor for downstream machine learning tasks such as biosynthetic class prediction, structure-activity correlation, or natural product database. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/biosynformatic-descriptor-computation))
- **biosynformatic-vector-computation**: Use when you have a natural product molecule structure in SMILES, InChI, or SDF format and need to convert it into a fixed-length numerical vector representation (fingerprint) that preserves biosynthetic and chemical information for machine learning, similarity searching, or class prediction tasks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/biosynformatic-vector-computation))
- **biosynthetic-class-count-tabulation**: Use when reproducing or summarizing repository-level counts from MIBiG's statistics page — e.g. totals per biosynthetic class or per taxon from the 2021 curated BGCs in MIBiG 2.0. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-class-count-tabulation))
- **biosynthetic-discrepancy-classification**: Use when you have RAIChU-predicted product scaffolds plus characterised SMILES for the same PKS/NRPS/NRPS-PKS hybrid BGCs and need to systematically explain why predictions diverge from the real natural product. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-discrepancy-classification))
- **biosynthetic-diversity-global-map-visualization**: Use when you have a completed BiG-SLiCE clustering run (SQLite3 output, e.g. the pre-processed full_run_result.zip of ~1.2M microbial BGCs) and want to chart BGC class distribution and GCF novelty across GTDB taxonomy. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-diversity-global-map-visualization))
- **biosynthetic-domain-curation-via-ec-annotation**: Use when tokenised BGCs (antiSMASH-detected, (sub)Pfam-annotated) contain non-biosynthetic domains that must be removed prior to sub-cluster prediction or redundancy filtering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-domain-curation-via-ec-annotation))
- **biosynthetic-domain-module-sampling**: Use when you need an unbiased benchmark set of modular PKS/NRPS cluster architectures (NRPS, cis-AT PKS, trans-AT PKS, hybrid PKS types) to stress-test automated drawing, prediction, or annotation pipelines on combinatorial module compositions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-domain-module-sampling))
- **biosynthetic-domain-pfam-selection**: Use when constructing or curating the domain feature space for BGC feature fingerprinting — e.g., selecting core biosynthetic Pfam pHMMs for BiG-SLiCE runs, or rebuilding/validating the sub-Pfam (sublevel) signature model library. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-domain-pfam-selection))
- **biosynthetic-gene-block-extraction**: Use when the input is antiSMASH GenBank (.gbk) output for a biosynthetic gene cluster (e.g. from MIBiG) and collinear gene blocks with their module/domain composition and a valid module ordering must be extracted for downstream PKS/NRPS visualisation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-block-extraction))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-annotation**: Use when when you have BGC sequences in FASTA or GenBank format and need to identify and extract biosynthetic Pfam domains for feature-based clustering, similarity search, or functional characterization. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-annotation))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-annotation-parsing**: Use when antiSMASH GenBank outputs must be converted into ordered per-gene Pfam domain strings with Core Biosynthetic gene annotations, e.g. to seed LCS-based BGC similarity comparisons. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-annotation-parsing))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-detection**: Use when mining assembled bacterial (or fungal) genome FASTA files for BGCs and needing reduced false positive rates or detection of BGC classes unseen during training, especially when ClusterFinder or antiSMASH yields too many loose predictions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-detection))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-detection**: Use when comparing metagenome assemblies by biologically meaningful content — counting BGCs (alongside Swiss-Prot CDSs, viral sequences, and CheckM/Prokka scaffold categories) recovered per assembler per sample. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-detection))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-detection-and-annotation**: Use when you have assembled microbial genomes (nucleotide FASTA files) and want to identify biosynthetic potential and group related BGCs for downstream linking with metabolomic data; ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v1/skills/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-detection-and-annotation) · 2 copies)
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-error-detection**: Use when auditing MIBiG or similar BGC records whose linked natural-product structure (PubChem or NP Atlas SMILES) may mismatch the cluster's annotated biosynthesis, e.g. before reusing characterised BGC-structure pairs for benchmarking or training. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-error-detection))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-euclidean-projection**: Use when you need to cluster large numbers of antiSMASH-predicted BGCs into GCFs (e.g., &gt;100,000 BGCs) and pairwise network tools like BiG-SCAPE are computationally infeasible due to O(n²) runtime. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-euclidean-projection))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-family-networking**: Use when you have antiSMASH GenBank files for training-set and sample genomes and need a BGC-by-genome similarity matrix (scores 0–1, 0.7 cutoff) to feed a KNN classifier or define GCFs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-family-networking))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-genome-mining**: Use when you have a set of genome assemblies (e.g., 170 S. mutans strains) and need to predict specialized-metabolism gene clusters before quantifying their abundance in metagenomes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-genome-mining))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-genome-mining**: Use when you have complete strain genomes (e.g. RefSeq FASTA) and need per-genome counts of BGCs, RiPP-encoding clusters, and short ORF candidates under motif-ORF vs all-ORF mining modes to benchmark mining sensitivity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-genome-mining))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-metabolite-annotation**: Use when curating or re-annotating biosynthetic gene cluster (BGC) entries that carry compound-structure or substrate SMILES strings, and you need to verify SMILES syntax correctness before release. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-metabolite-annotation))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-metadata-extraction**: Use when you need to enumerate or audit evidence codes and annotations (e.g. 13 NRPS substrate-selectivity evidence codes) directly from the MIBiG JSON schema or its entry JSON files. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-metadata-extraction))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-mining-with-genomic-data**: Use when you have LC-MS/MS spectra (MGF, mzXML, mzML, or mzData format) from a bacterial or fungal strain and corresponding genomic sequence data (FASTA, antiSMASH .final.gbk, or BOA .annotated. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-mining-with-genomic-data) · 3 copies)
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-pairwise-distance-computation**: Use when you have antiSMASH-processed GenBank files (or MIBiG reference BGCs) and need pairwise BGC distances to build a similarity network or call GCFs, especially across large, mixed complete/fragmented BGC sets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-pairwise-distance-computation))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-prediction**: Use when you have annotated genome assemblies (.gbk) across a genus-scale dataset and need BGC predictions, GCF clustering at a defined distance cutoff, and a binary GCF-by-genome matrix for lifestyle association testing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-prediction))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-prediction-antismash**: Use when starting from complete or draft genome assemblies (GenBank/WGS files) and BGC predictions are needed as the entry point for a comparative biosynthesis workflow such as BiG-SCAPE GCF clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-prediction-antismash))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-random-generation**: Use when you need synthetic PKS/NRPS/hybrid BGCs of controlled module count (e.g. 1-10 modules, 10 BGCs per type) to benchmark rendering speed, validate drawing correctness/readability, or stress-test biosynthetic pathway visualization pipelines. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-random-generation))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-similarity-network-analysis**: Use when antiSMASH-predicted BGC GenBank files (plus optional MIBiG reference BGCs) need to be organized into sequence similarity networks and GCFs/GCCs to map biosynthetic diversity across large genome sets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-similarity-network-analysis))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-spaghetti-diagram-layout**: Use when a predicted or MIBiG-derived modular PKS/NRPS BGC needs an automated, readable pathway visualisation at module resolution (spaghetti diagram in SVG), or when manually drawn biosynthetic models are too laborious/error-prone. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-spaghetti-diagram-layout))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-tokenization**: Use when you have GenBank-formatted BGC records and need to prepare them for sub-cluster detection, domain pattern analysis, or comparison across multiple BGCs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-tokenization))
- **biosynthetic-gene-cluster-window-construction**: Use when assembled nucleotide contigs must be partitioned into putative RiPP-encoding BGC regions, by anchoring fixed-size (10 kbp) windows on HMMer-detected modification enzyme hits before candidate ORF extraction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-cluster-window-construction))
- **biosynthetic-gene-domain-tokenisation**: Use when raw antiSMASH BGC annotations must be converted into per-gene protein-family tokens before statistical or topic-model sub-cluster mining, and clade-level subPfam resolution is needed to differentiate related domains. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-domain-tokenisation))
- **biosynthetic-gene-to-cluster-mapping**: Use when MEANtools/ClusterONE has produced overlapping feature clusters from a paired transcriptome-metabolome dataset and you need to annotate which FCs contain biosynthetic genes from known pathways to validate clustering or generate hypotheses. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-gene-to-cluster-mapping))
- **biosynthetic-region-annotation**: Use when a genome assembly must be searched for candidate BGC regions responsible for known or orphan metabolites, and sub-cluster motifs (e.g. methylaminosugar, (amino)deoxysugar, halogenated aromatic ring) are needed to prioritise the region. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-region-annotation))
- **biosynthetic-signal-separation-analysis**: Use when you need to validate that a BGC feature-space embedding (e.g., BiG-SLiCE GCF models at T = 900) preserves biosynthetic signal, by testing whether within-MIBiG-group distances separate from between-group distances. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-signal-separation-analysis))
- **biosynthetic-substructure-curated-annotation**: Use when you have predicted sub-cluster motifs (e.g. PRESTO-TOP LDA topics over tokenised BGCs) and a characterised reference set (SubClusterBlast sub-clusters, MIBiG) and need to link motifs to chemical substructures for genome-based structure prediction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/biosynthetic-substructure-curated-annotation))
